# 05 · Engineering Changelog

**Canonical source for: what every PR changed and why.** Engineering/security log — not
product release notes. **Every PR must add an entry** (or justify omission per
[09_DOCS_UPDATE rules in 08](./08_CODE_AND_DOCS_STANDARD.md)). Newest first. Seeded only
from PRs verified via `git log` / `gh pr list`.

---

### Slack PR 12 — hosted connector runner SKELETON (typed app↔runner seam, fail-closed) · 2026-06-28
- **Scope:** the typed boundary the future hosted runner implements — NOT an in-repo runner (doc 46 §11 PINS the runner as a SEPARATE deployable; the app stays pg-free; in-repo pg runner forbidden). New `src/lib/server/connector-vault/runner-ingest-entrypoint.ts` (server-only) defines `RunnerIngestEntrypoint` (`run(request, deps?)`), `RunnerIngestRequest` (non-secret envelope, NO plaintext), `RunnerIngestResult` (redacted), the closed `RunnerIngestReason` (reuses the harness `IngestReason` + skeleton guard reasons), pure `validateRunnerIngestRequest`, `isRunnerIngestEntrypointEnabled` (always false — `productionRunnerReady` hardcoded false), and `createDisabledRunnerIngestEntrypoint()` whose `run()` always returns `{ok:false, reason:'disabled'}` — no token load, no pg/KMS/AWS/RunnerConnection instantiated, no request fields logged. The ONLY import is a single TYPE import from the committed core, so the future separate runner is a zero-contract-change drop-in.
- **Guardrails / boundary scans:** new `scripts/check-app-runtime-imports.sh` (+selftest) — app stays pg-free, no `@aws-sdk/client-secretsmanager`, `src/app` imports no runner internals/KMS client, `@aws-sdk/client-kms` confined to the two committed adapters; extended connector-vault `no-client-import` (entrypoint not importable from `src/app`/`use client`) + `no-disk` (only `node:crypto`+relative, no fs/tmpdir sink) scans. No migration, no new dependency, no service-role. **Tests:** runner-ingest-entrypoint (guard always false; run always fail-closed `disabled`; per-reason validation; no input/token leak; type-only import) + the three scans. Tests 893 → 897.
- **RISK-007 remains OPEN** — this is a gate, not closure. `VaultProviderTokenSource` stays fail-closed. Closure still needs the separate hosted runner (ECS/Fargate + Secrets Manager, doc 46 §11/§12), provisioned+verified prod KMS/IAM (doc 44 §0), the doc 44 §5 first-real-token dry-run, a reviewed `connector_runner_login` provisioning (doc 45), and the B2c-run runbook. RISK-001 also remains OPEN; customer-production Slack BLOCKED.

### Slack PR 11 — production credential path FOUNDATION (vault token source, fail-closed) · 2026-06-27
- **Scope:** moves the token model toward "OAuth/vault/runner source → existing Slack chain" while keeping all risky production enablement DISABLED. New `vault-provider-token-source.ts` — `createVaultProviderTokenSource()`, a **typed FAIL-CLOSED placeholder** (same `ProviderTokenSource` seam; `getProviderToken` always throws a generic error; no token loading, no fake, no fallback; `isVaultProviderTokenSourceEnabled` always false). New `provider-token-source-selector.ts` — `createProviderTokenSource(env)` chooses the dev source ONLY in local-dev+opt-in, else the fail-closed vault source, with **NO vault→dev fallback**. The 3 chain callers (scheduler, internal trigger, manual-run entrypoint) now select the token source by env.
- **Why a placeholder (verified, not assumed):** all 7 hard-stops apply — no hosted runner (in-repo runner forbidden, doc 46 §11), production KMS/IAM unverified (doc 44 §0), OAuth callback fully synthetic+production-disabled, the doc 44 §5 first-real-token dry-run (17-item proof) not run, RISK-007 OPEN. The future swap is documented (OAuth→exchange→`connector_secrets` envelope→hosted runner `loadConnectorSecret`/`acquireRunnerDecryptCapability`/KMS provider) but nothing from the vault/runner/KMS/AWS layer is imported or instantiated.
- **Guardrails:** allowlist-shaped, env-only (request can't enable); no service-role; no migration; no production touch; the dev token env is read by the dev source ONLY (static-scanned); vault source + selector read no token env; safe generic errors (no token/env/request leaked). **Tests:** vault always-fail-closed + enable-flag-always-false + no-leak + imports-nothing-risky; selector dev-only-in-local-dev + every-other-env→vault + **no fallback** + request-can't-enable; server-only scan. Tests 885 → 893.
- **RISK-007 remains OPEN** — closure still needs the hosted runner (separate deployable), provisioned+verified production KMS/IAM, the doc 44 §5 dry-run, B2c-run first-real-token (doc 45), and the `connector_runner_login` migration. Customer-facing Connect-Slack still NOT ready (no real OAuth, no real token, no runner, no verified prod KMS/IAM). RISK-001 also remains OPEN.

### Slack P0 PR 10 — scheduler/retry worker (LOCAL/DEV/INTERNAL ONLY) · 2026-06-27
- **Scope:** a guarded scheduler/retry worker for the Slack sync, reusing the PR #194/#195 lifecycle + lock + stale-reconcile. Shape = a worker tick `runSlackSyncSchedulerTick` (eligibility + per-target `recordedSlackSyncRun`) + a **cron-secret + env-flag gated** POST route `/api/internal/slack-scheduler` (thin wrapper over the testable `handleSlackSchedulerRequest`; PRODUCTION-DISABLED, returns 404 when off; NOT public-unauthenticated). **Production cron infra (Vercel cron) is deferred, not wired.**
- **Guardrails:** `isSlackSchedulerEnabled` (allowlist: local dev + `ID_CADDIE_SLACK_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=1`; prod/preview/unknown refuse; request can't enable) + a constant-time `ID_CADDIE_SLACK_SCHEDULER_SECRET` header. **Write identity = the dev-user-JWT client (user-scoped RLS, NEVER service-role)** — §8 honored, no service-role shortcut. Eligibility = an explicit tenant-scoped connector allowlist (`SLACK_SYNC_TENANT_ID` + `ID_CADDIE_SLACK_SCHEDULER_CONNECTORS`); never "run all tenants". Retry/backoff: non-retryable auth/scope failures NOT retried; transient/success eligible after a 30-min backoff; no in-tick loop; duplicates → `run_already_active` (no Slack call); stale → reconciled by PR #195. Credential = dev token source (#187) only; no customer/production credentials. Safe results only (connector label + status/error/counts; no token/JWT/email/name/raw).
- **Test evidence:** `slack-sync-scheduler.test.ts` — env-guard matrix; eligibility/retry/backoff matrix; target parsing; constant-time secret; route handler 404/401/200/safe-500; tick orchestration (disabled→no work, eligible→runs, ineligible→skipped, duplicate→`run_already_active`, no leak) + the server-only static scan. No migration/RLS change (write path = PR #194/#195 recorder, RLS proven by Test 59/60 + recorder IT).
- **Out of scope (unsafe/incomplete):** OAuth/vault/runner production credential source (RISK-007 OPEN); customer-facing Connect-Slack; production scheduler enablement (Vercel cron) deferred; first-class connector lifecycle/status; Google Workspace; value-layer/license/spend. Tests 868 → 884. RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### Slack P0 PR 9 — controlled internal run-trigger exposure · 2026-06-27
- **Category:** Slack P0 sync — controlled internal trigger (no scheduler/retry/OAuth/runner/KMS/Connect-Slack/production/service-role; RISK-007 stays OPEN). A `"use server"` action (`internal/slack-sync/actions.ts`) over a server-only orchestrator (`internal-slack-trigger.ts`), invoked from a **hidden, flag-gated internal page** (`internal/slack-sync/page.tsx`, **not in nav**). Runs in a request context so it uses the **cookie `createClient()`** (the authenticated operator's user-scoped client) — RLS as that user, never service-role/dev-JWT.
- **Guard/authz:** `isInternalSlackTriggerEnabled` (allowlist: local dev + distinct opt-in `ID_CADDIE_INTERNAL_SLACK_TRIGGER_ENABLED=1`; prod/preview/unknown/unset refuse; request can't enable) + `authorizeInternalTrigger` (authenticated user + single active write-role tenant from `resolveTenantContext`; tenant never request-supplied; refuses unauthenticated/no-tenant/multi-tenant/viewer). Reuses the existing chain via `recordedSlackSyncRun` (no duplicated logic) — a refused or duplicate (`run_already_active`) trigger never calls Slack/emitter/resolver. Safe `RunSlackSyncSummary` returned (no token/JWT/email/name/raw); surfaced via the page's RLS-scoped "Last run" status.
- **No migration, no new RLS** (write path = PR #194/#195 recorder, RLS proven by Test 59/60 + recorder IT; this adds an app-layer authz gate). UI: hidden internal-dev page, "Internal dev Slack sync" label, button only when flag-on + write-role; no Connect-Slack/OAuth/scheduler/production language. Tests: env-guard matrix + authz matrix + orchestration (chain called with auth-derived tenant; refused→no chain; duplicate passthrough; safe summary) + page/action static scan. Tests 857 → 868. RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### Slack P0 PR 8 — concurrent-run locking + stale-run reconciliation · 2026-06-27
- **Category:** Slack P0 sync — run locking + stale reconciliation (no scheduler/retry-worker/OAuth/runner/KMS/Connect-Slack/service-role/production; RISK-007 stays OPEN). Migration **0038**: a partial unique index `manual_sync_runs (tenant_id, source, connector_id) where status='running'` — at most one active run per (tenant, source, connector), **tenant-scoped** (no cross-tenant block), DB-enforced (no check-then-insert race). Additive (CREATE INDEX only); gen-types 0-diff.
- **Behavior:** the recorder `start()` returns `{ok:false, reason:'run_already_active'}` on the 23505 lock conflict (no record created); the wrapper refuses the duplicate and **never calls the Slack client/emitter/resolver**. Inline stale reconciliation (no scheduler) before each run: `reconcileStaleRuns` marks runs stuck in `running` past `STALE_RUN_MS` (30 min) as `failed`/`error_code='stale_run_reconciled'` with `finished_at` — no invented counts, no token/JWT/email/name/raw; the 0037 immutability trigger permits running→failed; completed runs stay immutable; created_by integrity holds; tenant-scoped via RLS.
- **UI:** no change — PR 7's "Last Slack sync" section already renders running/succeeded/failed; a stale run shows as failed with `stale_run_reconciled`. **Tests:** recorder/wrapper units (lock conflict, reconcile, duplicate makes no chain call) + real DB/RLS `org_rls_test.sql` Test 60 (partial-index lock; tenant-scoped; lock released) + a TS recorder IT (second run refused/not recorded; A doesn't block B; stale reconciled then new run starts; A cannot reconcile B). Tests 852 → 857. RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### Slack P0 PR 7 — run lifecycle/status for the manual Slack sync · 2026-06-27
- **Category:** Slack P0 sync — run lifecycle/status (tenant-scoped audit record; no scheduler/OAuth/runner/KMS/Connect-Slack/service-role/production; RISK-007 stays OPEN). New table `manual_sync_runs` (migration **0037**, additive + RLS) — distinct from the OAuth-scoped `connector_runs` (0017); `connector_id` is a LABEL, not an FK. Fields: tenant_id, source(=slack), connector_id, status(running|succeeded|failed), started_at, finished_at, error_code, failed_stage, the 8 counts, created_by(`default auth.uid()`), timestamps — only SAFE aggregates (never token/JWT/email/name/raw). RLS: members read (`is_tenant_member`), owner/admin/editor write (`has_tenant_role`), no delete (append-only).
- **Write path:** `manual-sync-run-recorder.ts` (`createSupabaseManualSyncRunRecorder`, injected user-scoped client, never service-role) start()→running, finish()→succeeded/failed from the safe summary; `recorded-slack-sync-run.ts` wraps the chain (refused runs make no record; start before, finish after; a crash leaves `running`, never misleading-success). The live entrypoint now records. **Read/UI:** `getLatestSlackSyncRunForCurrentTenant` (RLS-scoped safe DTO) drives a read-only "Last Slack sync" section on the app detail page — status/last-success/error/counts, empty state, no run/connect button, no scheduler language.
- **Concurrent-run locking deferred (next PR):** no existing safe pattern; a DB lock needs stale-run reconciliation. **Real DB/RLS:** `org_rls_test.sql` Test 59 (member read; owner/admin/editor write; viewer read-only; cross-tenant isolation; created_by default) + a TS recorder IT (member writes; cross-tenant denial) via `npm run test:store-it` + CI. gen-types regenerated (new table). Tests 840 → 852 (+ recorder IT skipped in normal runs). RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### Slack P0 PR 6 — manual server-only run trigger (LOCAL/DEV ONLY) · 2026-06-26
- **Category:** Slack P0 sync — manual run wiring (LOCAL/DEV ONLY). Wires #187→#188→#189→#190 into one run via `src/lib/server/sync/run-slack-sync-dev.ts` `runSlackSyncDev({env, tokenSource, httpClient, store, identity, observedAt})` → safe aggregate summary (counts/booleans; no token/email/name/raw; safe `errorCode` on failure). Allowlist-shaped run guard `isDevSlackSyncRunEnabled` (local dev + distinct opt-in `ID_CADDIE_DEV_SLACK_SYNC_ENABLED=1`; unknown/unset/test/staging/preview/prod refuse; request can't enable). User-scoped context = `dev-user-scoped-client.ts` (dev tenant-member JWT in Authorization over the **public anon key** — RLS, never service-role; JWT never logged). Concrete store = `supabase-slack-resolver-store.ts` (#190 seam over the injected user-scoped client; 0036 onConflict upserts; people get-or-create on the functional index; matches DO NOTHING no-repoint; safe `store_write_failed`). Trigger = guarded `.mjs` pre-flight (`scripts/run-slack-sync-dev.mjs`) — refuses outside local dev / prod ref / token-in-argv; prints the procedure (no route/action/UI button).
- **Real committed run command:** `npm run slack:sync:dev` (`run-slack-sync-dev.liverun.test.ts` via vitest — the repo's TS runner; plain node can't resolve the chain, no tsx) builds the real deps (token source + `slackFetchHttpClient` + `createSupabaseSlackResolverStore(createDevUserScopedClient(env))`) and invokes `runSlackSyncDev`, printing only the safe summary. Double-gated (`ID_CADDIE_DEV_SLACK_SYNC_ENABLED=1` AND `SLACK_SYNC_LIVE=1`) → skipped in `npm test`/CI.
- **Real DB/RLS proof of the TS store (RUN, not mocks):** `supabase-slack-resolver-store.it.test.ts` runs the store's supabase-js/PostgREST queries against a LOCAL Supabase stack as a tenant-member JWT (service-role for fixture setup only) — 0036-key idempotent upsert, full graph write, get-or-create `_`-literal match, and RLS cross-tenant write DENIAL (`store_write_failed`). `npm run test:store-it` (`scripts/test-store-it.sh` → `supabase start` + vitest) + CI `store-integration.yml`. SQL `org_rls_test.sql` Test 58 still proves the constraints/RLS independently. Added `supabase/config.toml` (local stack, alternate ports).
- **Safe resolver-failure diagnostics (added after the first live run returned a bare `resolve_failed`):** failures now return `{ failedStage, table, safeDbCode, safeReason, usersFetched, factsEmitted, factsRejected }`. The store attaches a value-free `StoreWriteFailure = { table, op, code }` — only the SQLSTATE/PostgREST `code`, never the DB message (which embeds emails). `safeReason`: `42501→rls_denied`, `2350x/23514→constraint_violation`, `42703/42P01/PGRST204/205→schema_mismatch`, else `unknown`. Unit-tested (mapping + no token/email/name/raw in the diagnostic); the store IT confirms a real cross-tenant RLS denial surfaces code `42501`. Live-run analysis: the operator's failing run wrote 0 rows to tenant `7a296850-…` ⇒ `upsert_app` RLS-denied ⇒ the dev-user JWT lacks owner/admin/editor membership in `SLACK_SYNC_TENANT_ID`.
- **Live end-to-end run — SUCCEEDED (operator, 2026-06-27, local/dev):** `npm run slack:sync:dev` ran the full chain → safe summary `ok:true, teamPresent:true, usersFetched:1, factsEmitted:6, factsRejected:0, appUsersWritten:1, peopleWritten:1, matchesWritten:1, matchConflicts:0, skipped:2` (no token/JWT/email/name/raw). Rows visible via the PR-5 UI; idempotent on re-run. Needed operator-only secrets (rotated dev Slack token + dev tenant-member JWT + dev workspace), so the agent could not run it. NOT customer OAuth, NOT the production runner, NOT a scheduler. No migration/types/dependency. Tests 803 → 840 (+4 real-DB IT + 1 live entrypoint, skipped in CI). RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### Slack P0 PR 5 — read-only UI display of synced Slack data · 2026-06-27
- **Category:** Slack P0 sync — read-only UI (no DB writes, no resolver, no staging, no trigger/scheduler/Slack-call/token/OAuth/runner/KMS, no service-role). Extends the EXISTING app detail page (`apps/[id]/page.tsx`, already renders the RLS-scoped app_users roster) with a **"Synced from Slack" marker** + a **"Synced Slack users" read-only-preview** reframing when the app is a connector-synced Slack workspace. New pure `slack-sync-display.ts` `classifySlackSync({externalInstanceId, vendorName})` (Slack-synced = external_instance_id present AND vendor "Slack" — structural marker, not display-name-only) + safe copy (no "Connect Slack"/"Run sync"/OAuth/"production" — read-only preview, "Manual run coming next").

No new RLS test needed (the markers are on the already-RLS-scoped apps row; the roster read is the existing RLS-proven path — org_rls_test T25/T28/T29).

Tests 793 → 803. No live Slack call, no real token, no customer credential, no DB writes, no production. RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### Slack P0 PR 4 — discovery facts → tenant-scoped resolver write path (DEEP GATE) · 2026-06-26
- **Category:** Slack P0 sync — resolver write path (no UI/trigger/scheduler/Slack-call/OAuth/runner/KMS/service-role). New server-only `slack-resolver-write.ts` `applySlackDiscoveryResolution(store, authTenantId, facts)` turns validated PR #189 facts into graph rows: apps → app_users → people → app_user_identity_matches, via the injected user-scoped store pattern (concrete Supabase impl deferred, matching resolver-write.ts/identity-match-write.ts). Direct resolver (facts already validated; not staging-first). Writes apps/app_users/people/app_user_identity_matches; NOT identity_accounts (RLS default-deny).
- **Migration `0036` (staging, additive + defensive preflight):** tenant-scoped natural keys for idempotent upsert — `apps UNIQUE(tenant_id, external_instance_id)`, `app_users UNIQUE(tenant_id, app_id, external_user_id)`, `people UNIQUE(tenant_id, lower(primary_email))`. Tenant scope in EVERY key (no global provider uniqueness). No RLS change/widening, no service-role. gen-types 0-diff (constraints don't change columns).
- **Tenant isolation + idempotency are DB-enforced and proven with REAL DB/RLS tests** (`org_rls_test.sql` Test 58, acting as the non-superuser `authenticated` role): keys exist; ON CONFLICT re-run doesn't duplicate; a DIRECT duplicate INSERT is REFUSED by the DB constraint; same external id in two tenants stays separate; a Tenant A user cannot write a Tenant B row (RLS WITH CHECK denies). tenant_id ALWAYS from authTenantId (spoofed payload tenant_id skipped). Exact-email-only matching (no fuzzy); email optional (app_user without email still writes; person/match only when email); role_admin has no column → safe role label in app_users.raw_payload provenance (documented gap); usage → app_users.last_active_at; never persists token/auth-header/raw-Slack-object.
- **Tests:** 12 unit (in-memory store models the natural keys) + Test 58 real RLS. RLS suite 631 → 637; tests 780 → 792. Schema gaps documented (no role column; no app_instances table; identity_accounts default-deny). No live Slack call, no real token, no customer credential, no UI/trigger/scheduler/OAuth/runner/KMS, no production. RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### Slack P0 PR 3 — Slack discovery-fact emitter · 2026-06-26
- **Category:** Slack P0 sync (no DB/resolver/UI/manual-trigger/OAuth/runner/KMS; no Slack call). New server-only `src/lib/server/connector-vault/slack-discovery-emitter.ts` mirrors `okta-discovery-emitter.ts`: maps the PR #188 normalized records (auth.test workspace + `SlackUserRecord[]`) → validated discovery facts → **safe fact array** (stops before staging). `source_type=deep_provider_sync`, `source_provider=slack`; tenant_id ALWAYS from the authenticated arg (payload tenant_id never read), observed_at from the caller; allowlist construction (no raw spread), every candidate re-validated via `parseDiscoveryFact` (strict), malformed/bot records skipped without failing the batch.
- **Facts:** app_discovery (once), app_instance_identity (once, team_id anchor), app_user_account (per non-bot, always — even without email), person_identity_candidate (ONLY when email; signal id = normalized lower-cased email; never constructed when email missing), role_admin (admin/owner/primary_owner, priority primary_owner>owner>admin; plain members get role_hint only), usage_activity (only with a real last-activity ts). `has_2fa`/`has_sso` never read (live-verified unavailable). Deterministic signal ids (no random/timestamp). 16 synthetic tests + registered server-only in no-client-import. Tests 763 → 779.
- **No DB writes, no resolver, no live Slack call, no UI/manual-trigger/OAuth/runner/KMS, no customer credential, no real token, no production, no new dependency, no migration/types.** Resolver write path is the next deep-gate PR. RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### Slack P0 PR 2 — server-only Slack API client (verified in isolation) · 2026-06-26
- **Category:** Slack P0 sync (no DB/discovery-fact/resolver/UI/OAuth/runner/KMS). New server-only `src/lib/server/sync/slack/slack-client.ts`: `createSlackClient({tokenSource,httpClient,identity},{includeBots})` → `authTest()`+`listUsers()`. Token ONLY via the PR #187 seam (no direct env read); Slack reached ONLY via the INJECTED httpClient (no global fetch) with the token in the Authorization header (never URL/log/record/error). P0 calls `auth.test`+`users.list` (single workspace; scopes `users:read`,`users:read.email`; Enterprise Grid/`admin.users.list` deferred). Handles cursor pagination (`response_metadata.next_cursor`, 100-page cap), `ok:false`/invalid_auth/missing_scope, 429/Retry-After, malformed/missing-field fail-closed, deleted/restricted/ultra-restricted explicit, **bots filtered by default** (incl. USLACKBOT).
- **Normalized `SlackUserRecord`** = allowlisted non-secret fields only (no raw spread, no token/headers/full-response/tenant_id-from-Slack; rawProvenance = {updated,tzOffset,color}). 24 mocked tests (no network): auth/users/pagination/ok-false/invalid-auth/missing-scope/429/malformed/email-present-absent/bot-filter/deleted-restricted/no-raw-spread/no-token-leak/global-fetch-never-called/server-only-boundary + Retry-After sanitization (positive int only) + repeating-cursor break (no duplicate-record loop) + the dev-verify guard. Tests 730 → 759.
- **Live field-path verification RUN (2026-06-26, disposable workspace, safe aggregates only)** via `scripts/verify-slack-field-paths-dev.mjs` (local-dev-only, allowlist-shaped, fails closed in CI/staging/prod; never prints token/email/name/raw/xoxb-). Result: auth.test+users.list ok, single page; PRESENT id/team_id/deleted/is_admin/is_owner/is_primary_owner/is_restricted/is_ultra_restricted/is_bot/tz/updated/profile.{display_name,real_name,title,status_text}. **Reconciled to the live shape:** `has_2fa`/`has_sso` are ABSENT in P0 users.list → removed from required output, **provenance-only when present** (2FA/SSO posture deferred to Enterprise Grid/SCIM); `profile.email` was ABSENT on the (small-sample, 1 non-bot) member → `email` is **optional** in the client (missing-email + present-email both tested), and PR 3 emits person_identity_candidate only when email exists. **PENDING before PR 3:** Sam re-verifies `profile.email` PRESENT on a member with a known email, or escalates as a blocker. **Rerun was still inconclusive** (withEmail 1 but the workspace has 2 bots + 1 non-bot → the email may be a bot's), so `verify-slack-field-paths-dev.mjs` now breaks email presence **by user type** (`botsWithEmail`/`nonBotsWithEmail`/`nonBotsMissingEmail`) and samples a non-bot WITH email for the field-path block (`pickSampledNonBot` returns booleans only — no raw member). **#188 email merge gate: `nonBotsWithEmail >= 1` AND `profile.email` PRESENT on the non-bot sample** — **CLOSED ✅** (final live run after adding a non-bot member with an email: nonBotsWithEmail 1, botsWithEmail 0, sampledNonBotWithEmailFound true, PRESENT profile.email on the non-bot sample; has_2fa/has_sso still ABSENT). Email is per-user optional (client never requires it); PR 3 emits person_identity_candidate only when email exists. The PR-3 email precondition is satisfied; #188 is merge-ready.
- **No Slack API client beyond auth.test/users.list, no live Slack call in CI/tests, no real token, no customer credential, no OAuth/runner/KMS/IAM, no DB/discovery-fact/resolver/UI, no production enablement, no new dependency, no migration/types.** RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### Slack P0 PR 1 — server-only dev-token source abstraction · 2026-06-26
- **Category:** Slack P0 sync scaffold (does NOT change RISK-007 posture). New server-only `src/lib/server/sync/provider-token-source.ts`: the `ProviderTokenSource.getProviderToken({provider,tenantId,connectorId,purpose})` seam future Slack sync depends on (the FUTURE OAuth/vault/runner source implements the same interface — `VaultProviderTokenSource` placeholder, not implemented) + a **dev/test-only** Slack implementation `createDevProviderTokenSource(env)` reading the token from server-only `ID_CADDIE_DEV_SLACK_TOKEN` (never `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`/code/DB/UI; returns only the in-memory `{provider,token}`; never logs/throws the token).
- **ALLOWLIST-shaped fail-closed guard** `isDevProviderTokenSourceEnabled`: enabled ONLY when positively local dev (`NODE_ENV==="development"` AND `VERCEL_ENV`∈{unset,development}) AND explicit opt-in `ID_CADDIE_DEV_PROVIDER_TOKEN_SOURCE_ENABLED==="1"` — unknown/unset/`test`/staging/preview/production all REFUSE (NOT deny-known-staging/prod). ### PR #179 — Production-shaped synthetic OAuth callback route (B2c-route) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2c-**route** (the production-shaped Slack OAuth callback route, fully synthetic + production-disabled). Deep-gated (public route + OAuth + session boundary). NO real Slack API call, NO real token, NO real client secret, NO request-path decrypt, NO route returning a decrypted token, NO live connector, NO production enablement. NO migration (tests 671 → **686**; RLS **620**; types **1955**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **`handleSyntheticSlackOAuthCallback(request, deps)`** (new server-only `oauth-callback-route-handler.ts`): the production request-path SHAPE — guard → explicit session resolution → parse → B2c-wire orchestrator → safe/static response — wired with FULLY SYNTHETIC/mocked deps.
- **Production-disabled guard (earliest refusal):** `isSyntheticCallbackEnabled` reads **trusted server env only** (false in production and without the explicit `CONNECTOR_OAUTH_SYNTHETIC_CALLBACK_ENABLED=1` staging opt-in; a request value cannot enable it). When disabled, the handler returns a **generic 404 BEFORE reading/parsing/logging any request material** — it does not reveal an OAuth callback, name the guard/env/config, or return a reason; the orchestrator is not called.
- **Explicit session resolution (no layout auth):** the handler resolves the completing subject via an injected `resolveSubject` (the route wires `getSessionUser`); no session / a resolution failure fails closed to a safe error. **Wrong session** (subject ≠ `state.sub`) is rejected by B2a (`subject_mismatch`) and never reaches the exchange.
- **Request-path discipline + safe responses:** only `state` + `code` are read from the query — never logged, echoed into an error, put in a response body/header, or returned. Responses are safe/static **303s to `/connectors?oauth=success|error`** (a coarse flag only — never raw code/state/reason). An orchestrator throw → safe error.
- **Upstream redirect discipline (proxy.ts — review fold):** the Proxy/middleware `updateSession` redirected an unauthenticated request for a protected route to `/login` while **preserving the original query** — so `/connectors/oauth/callback?code=X&state=Y` became `/login?code=X&state=Y`, leaking the raw `code`/`state` into the `/login` URL (access logs, history, referer, analytics) **before** the handler guard runs. Fixed: the redirect now **drops the query** (`redirectUrl.search = ""`) — realizing the design intent that the in-flight `code`/`state` are dropped (`/login` reads only `?error=`). 3 `proxy.test.ts` tests prove the callback (and any protected route's query) redirects to a clean `/login`. No other upstream layer logs the callback URL/query (no `console`/logger touches `request.url`/`searchParams`; `@vercel/analytics`'s `<Analytics/>` is a page-layout client component, not run for the API route).
- **No egress / no client-secret decrypt:** the route wires `makeSyntheticOrchestratorRunner` (a synthetic http client returning a token-shaped `xoxb-…MUSTNOTLEAK…` sentinel, a synthetic client-secret sentinel, a synthetic store) — it has **no global `fetch`**, can never reach `slack.com`, and **does NOT import or call `withSlackClientSecret`/the B2c-secret decrypt boundary** (the synthetic exchange needs no real client-secret decrypt). The no-client-import guard now allows only the callback route to import the route handler.
- **Tests (synthetic — 12):** marked code/state/session/token/client-secret sentinels. Cover: production-disabled → generic 404, no disclosure, orchestrator not called, no request material logged; the trusted-env guard (production + missing opt-in disabled); no-session / session-failure → safe error, orchestrator not called; success/failure → safe 303 with no raw detail; an orchestrator throw → safe error; **end-to-end with the REAL orchestrator + synthetic deps** — a valid request reaches the (synthetic) exchange and **global fetch is never called**, while tampered state / wrong session / missing code do **NOT** reach the exchange (B2a/B2c-wire gate); an import-boundary scan (no `withSlackClientSecret`/`slack-client-secret-store`/decrypt/`fetch(`/`@slack/`).
- **Posture:** **B2c-route only — production-shaped but SYNTHETIC + production-disabled. No real Slack API call happened, no real token entered the system, no real client secret entered the system.** The **first real-token event remains FUTURE + explicitly authorized (B2c-run)**; **B2c-run remains future**. Live connector use remains **BLOCKED**. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90), 44 (§7 — B2c-route done), 41 (§104), 09, 00.
- **Validation (local):** `npm test` **686/686** (54 files); lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean; `check-auth-safety`/`check-migration-safety`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `test-rls.sh` **620** (no migration); `gen-types-local.sh` 0-diff (1955); `check-no-real-tokens.sh selftest` + `--all` pass; ref staging `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

### PR #178 — Vault-grade Slack client-secret store (B2c-secret) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2c-**secret** (the OAuth master credential's vault-grade store, synthetic only). Standalone, deep-gated. NO real Slack client secret, NO real token, NO Slack API call, NO production callback route, NO live connector, NO request-path decrypt, NO production enablement. Tests 657 → **671**; RLS **614 → 620** (T56); migration `0035` (new table → types **1901 → 1955**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.

New `connector_app_secrets` table (migration `0035`) keyed by `{app_env, provider, secret_kind, version}` — **NO tenant_id**, append-only/versioned. **RLS deny-all to the request path** (RLS-enabled, ZERO policies, `revoke all` from authenticated/anon); **runner column-scoped grant only** (the same `connector_runner` BYPASSRLS + KMS boundary as the bot-token vault). The plaintext is never returned, logged, audited, thrown, or persisted. Decrypt runs through the injected runner-backed store + KMS provider — not the web/request identity, arbitrary app code, or any route. `saveSlackClientSecret` stores the envelope only (no plaintext at rest, redacted ref).

Cover: app-scope AAD round-trip + staging≠production + wrong provider/kind/version fail closed; envelope-only at rest (no plaintext in row/return); the secret reaches **only** the exchange callback and appears in no result/log/error; **a forced post-decrypt exchange failure (the callback throws with the secret embedded) does not leak it** (fail-closed `use_failed`); an identity without `kms:Decrypt` cannot decrypt; a staging-sealed envelope cannot be used under a production identity; `not_found`; the runner-backed store INSERT goes under `SET ROLE connector_runner`, targets the granted columns, and carries ciphertext (not the plaintext); a runner round-trip; a source scan proving no `load/get/read/fetchClientSecret` API + no `process.env`.

### PR #176 — Synthetic OAuth callback wiring (B2c-wire) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2c-**wire** (synthetic end-to-end callback composition only). New server-only `oauth-callback-orchestrator.ts`. **B2c is split into FOUR never-combined steps: B2c-wire (this) / B2c-secret (real KMS client-secret store — future) / B2c-route (synthetic production callback route, request-path discipline proven — future) / B2c-run (first real-token event — future, explicitly authorized).** NO real Slack API call, NO real token, NO real client secret, NO route, NO request-path decrypt, NO live connector, NO production enablement. NO migration (tests 630 → **657**; RLS **614**; types **1901**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **`orchestrateSlackOAuthCallback(query, deps)`** composes the three already-proven pieces into ONE pure-function path: **B2a `validateOAuthState` (the authoritative GATE) → B2b `exchangeSlackOAuthCode` (MOCKED) → B1 store/encrypt**. It is a pure function — **no production OAuth callback route was added.**
- **Single source of truth:** after validation succeeds, every security-bound field threaded downstream (tenant, connector, exact redirect, correlation) comes ONLY from the **validated state payload** (`v.payload`). The untrusted callback query is read ONLY for `state` + `code` (asserted by a source scan); a decoy tenant/connector/provider in the query is ignored — proven by a test that puts decoys in the query and asserts the store binds the validated tenant/connector.
- **Causal gating:** `validateOAuthState`'s `ok` is the ONLY thing that unlocks the exchange (validation is authoritative, not advisory — its `payload` drives the flow). The exchange stage is unreachable for any state that did not pass validation (asserted: a tampered/invalid state → the injected http client has 0 calls). Stage ordering validate→exchange→store, each stage skipped if the prior fails.
- **Exchange-success/store-failure seam (orchestration layer):** when the mocked exchange succeeds (returns the token-shaped sentinel) but the store fails, the orchestrator returns a safe `store_failed` and the token appears nowhere (result/error/console) — the orchestrator never sees the token (B2b never returns it). Distinct from B1's internal DB/audit atomicity.
- **Real B1 path:** `b1StoreHandoff(deps)` wires the **real** `ingestStagingConnectorSecret` as the B2b store handoff; a test drives the token-shaped sentinel through the real envelope path and proves only **ciphertext** is stored (the sentinel never appears in the committed INSERT statements) and a redacted ref returns; a production-blocked B1 fails closed without leaking the token.
- **Tests (synthetic — 27):** use three detectable sentinels — token `xoxb-…MUSTNOTLEAK…`, a marked client-secret, a marked code — and the **REAL** B2a validator (states minted by `createOAuthState`). ### PR #175 — Mocked Slack OAuth exchange wrapper (B2b) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2b (mocked exchange wrapper only). New server-only `slack-oauth-exchange.ts`. NO real Slack API call, NO real token, NO callback route that performs a real exchange, NO live connector, NO request-path decrypt, NO production enablement. NO migration (tests 612 → **630**; RLS **614**; types **1901**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **`exchangeSlackOAuthCode(input, deps)`** (docs/42 §90.4): takes a **B2a-validated** authorization `code` + the EXACT redirect URI (caller precondition — never re-derived from request data), reads the client secret from an **INJECTED `ClientSecretProvider`** (NEVER `process.env`), POSTs `client_id/client_secret/code/redirect_uri` to the Slack token endpoint (`SLACK_TOKEN_URL` = `https://slack.com/api/oauth.v2.access`) via an **INJECTED `SlackHttpClient`**, parses the bot-token response in memory, and hands the token STRAIGHT to the B1 store/encrypt handoff (`ExchangeStoreHandoff`). Returns a **REDACTED ref** on success.
- **No leak:** the `code`, client secret, raw Slack response, and bot token are NEVER logged, echoed into an error, returned, or audited. Slack `error` codes are sanitized to safe static reason classes (never echoed). Fail-closed reasons: `missing_client_secret` / `client_secret_denied` / `exchange_http_error` / `slack_error` / `malformed_response` / `missing_bot_token` / `unexpected_token_type` / `store_failed`. The live token reference is dropped after the handoff (V8-heap residual remains — not a hard wipe, docs/44 §2 step 7).
- **No network:** the module has NO global `fetch` and NO fallback — the injected client is the only Slack-calling path. Tests stub global `fetch` to **fail loud** and assert it is never called, so an automated test cannot accidentally hit `slack.com`.
- **Tests (synthetic):** 18 tests use a **TOKEN-SHAPED** `xoxb-…MUSTNOTLEAK…` sentinel (a real Slack-`xoxb-`-shaped value carrying the approved synthetic marker — NOT a generic placeholder) so the no-leak assertions prove a real-shaped token would not survive into result/error/logs. Cover: valid response → store handoff + redacted ref (no token); `ok:false` sanitized; missing/non-bot token; malformed/non-object body; network/timeout; non-2xx; store failure; **missing/denied client secret** (provider throws — secret not surfaced even in the thrown error); forced post-secret-read failure doesn't surface the secret; **global fetch never called**; missing injected deps fail loud; the `code` is never logged/returned; extra Slack fields ignored; server-only source scan (no `fetch(`/`process.env`/decrypt/route/real-token).
- **Posture:** **B2b is a MOCKED exchange wrapper. No real Slack API call happened; no real token entered the system.** The **vault-grade/KMS-backed client-secret store remains FUTURE** (B2c — the provider is injected/mocked here); **exchange-specific audit (`connector_oauth.exchange.*`) remains FUTURE** (not implemented in B2b). **Real OAuth exchange remains BLOCKED; live connector use remains BLOCKED.** **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90.4), 44 (§7), 41 (§101), 09, 00.

### PR #174 — OAuth state binding + validation (B2a) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2a (state generation/validation only). Closes the docs/42 §90.2 `validateOAuthState` binding gap. NO Slack OAuth exchange, NO Slack API call, NO callback route that exchanges a code, NO real token, NO live connector, NO request-path decrypt, NO production enablement. Tests 585 → **612**; RLS **611 → 614** (T55); migration `0034` (0-diff types — `1901`); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **Eight-field binding (`oauth-state.ts`):** the signed payload now carries `redir` (EXACT redirect URI) + `corr` (correlation/operation id) in addition to `sub`/`tid`/`prov`/`cid`/`exp`/`nonce`. `createOAuthState` requires the actor subject + an absolute-HTTPS `redirectUri` + a grammar-safe `correlationId` (else throws, no state).
- **Per-field validation (`validateOAuthState`):** when a completing context is supplied it compares EACH bound field against the completing request/session and FAILS CLOSED on ANY mismatch — new safe reason codes `session_required` (no completing actor/session), `subject_mismatch`, `redirect_uri_mismatch`, `correlation_mismatch` (plus the existing tenant/provider/connector/expired/replayed/malformed). A state missing `sub`/`redir`/`corr` is `malformed_state`.
- **Generation-time authorization (`generateBoundOAuthState`):** an injected `authorizeActor` gate verifies the initiating actor is authorized for the bound tenant + connector — an actor CANNOT mint a state for a tenant/connector they cannot access, and the signer is **never invoked** on failure (no usable state). This prevents a self-consistent malicious state; the callback equality check prevents cross-state confusion; both are required.
- **Exact redirect, server-trusted (`serverTrustedRedirectUri`):** the redirect compared at callback is resolved ONLY from server config (absolute HTTPS), **NEVER** reconstructed from request-controlled inputs (Host, X-Forwarded-Host, Forwarded, request URL, origin, query). Tests prove a spoofed Host cannot make a mismatched redirect validate (it fails closed).
- **Atomic single-use reused:** the existing `oauth-pending-consume.ts` atomic consume (one `UPDATE … where consumed_at is null … returning`; concurrent second consume fails closed) is the single-use marker — no new race-prone code. Migration `0034` widens the `oauth_pending.last_rejected_code` CHECK for the four new codes (RLS **T55**: runner records them; an out-of-allowlist value is rejected).
- **Provider wiring:** `buildSlackAuthorizeUrl` / `persistSlackAuthorizePending` now require + bind the actor `subject`, the exact `redirectUri`, and a `correlationId` (the authorize redirect IS the bound redirect).
- **Tests (synthetic):** 26 new binding tests — generation binds all eight fields; generation-auth rejects an unauthorized tenant/connector; **one isolated per-field mismatch test ×8** (the other seven match, so removing any single comparison fails a specific test); confused-deputy (attacker-state/victim-session, tenant/connector/redirect/provider swap); expired; replayed; no-session/session_required; Host/X-Forwarded-Host spoof cannot satisfy redirect; safe/static reasons with no raw state/session in the result.
- **Posture:** **B2a is state generation/validation only. Slack exchange remains BLOCKED; a callback route that calls Slack remains BLOCKED; real token remains BLOCKED; live connector use remains BLOCKED; the Slack client-secret vault-grade store remains future/not implemented.** **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90.2), 44 (§7), 41 (§100), 09, 00.
- **Validation (local):** `npm test` **612/612** (49 files); lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean; `check-auth-safety`/`check-migration-safety`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `test-rls.sh` **614** (`ALL ORG-RLS ASSERTIONS PASSED`); `gen-types-local.sh` 0-diff (1901); `check-no-real-tokens.sh selftest` + `--all` pass; ref staging `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

### PR #173 — Slack OAuth authorize/callback/exchange design gate (B2, docs only) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 design gate (docs only — NO OAuth implementation, callback route, Slack API call, real token, live connector, request-path decrypt, or production enablement). Designs the Slack OAuth authorize→callback→code-exchange path in **doc 42 §90** + folds the threat/evidence/sequence into **doc 44** (§2/§5/§7). Tests/RLS/types unchanged (585 / 611 / 1901); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **doc 42 §90 (the design):** (1) **code-vs-token boundary** — the callback receives a one-time authorization `code` on the request path (NOT the token); it is validated + state-bound then handed to a SERVER-SIDE exchange; the `code`→token `oauth.v2.access` exchange is runner-only; the bot token is born server-side + encrypted IMMEDIATELY via the existing vault and NEVER returns toward the browser; no path exposes the token after exchange. (2) **state/CSRF/binding** — binds tenant/provider/connector + (the **B2a gap to close**) **actor/session (`sub`) + EXACT redirect URI** (no prefix/substring/origin/loose), single-use `nonce_hash`, short TTL — closes replay/CSRF/tenant-swap/connector-swap/wrong-user/victim-session/redirect-confusion/stale-code. The current `validateOAuthState` binds tenant/provider/connector but NOT `sub`/intent — flagged for B2a. (3) **Slack client secret = VAULT-GRADE** — KMS-backed, runner-only, ≥ bot-token strength, NEVER a plaintext env var (it mints tokens → its leak is app-wide). (4) **exchange path** (`oauth.v2.access`, runner identity, no token/response logging, sanitized errors, fail-closed, bot-token-only, refresh out of scope). (5) **receipt-to-encryption zero-observer plan** (access-log/Referer surfaces for the `code`; no server tracer/error-vendor exists). (6) **audit events** (authorize/callback/exchange + store) — never raw code/response/token/secret. (7) **fail-closed failure modes**. (8) **sequence** B2a→B2b→B2c→B2d.
- **doc 44 updates:** §2 — the **three secrets traced separately** (auth `code`, Slack token, client secret); §7 — the PR sequence **split B2 into B2 design → B2a (state validation + actor/exact-redirect binding) → B2b (mock exchange) → B2c (first real-token event, explicitly authorized) → B2d (live connector)**; §5 (OAuth sub-block) — the pre-first-OAuth evidence gate (staging flag, dev workspace/app, source-revocable token, named revocation operator, no-production/exact redirect URI, log surfaces, scanner, **client secret vault-grade + not in plaintext env**, runbooks, explicit Sam authorization).
- **Posture:** **B2 design only; OAuth implementation remains blocked; real token remains blocked; the first real-token event (B2c) remains future + explicitly authorized; live connector use remains blocked; the Slack client secret is vault-grade and must be protected before real exchange.** **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90), 44 (§2/§5/§7), 09, 00, 41 (§99).
- **Validation (local):** `npm test` **585/585**; lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `check-auth-safety`/`check-docs-updated`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `check-no-real-tokens.sh selftest` + `--all` pass; docs-only (no migration/types/RLS — RLS 611, types 1901); ref staging `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

### PR #172 — Staging-only store/encrypt ingestion path (B1, synthetic only) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B1 (the **store/encrypt half** of doc 44 §2, **synthetic only**). New server-only `connector-secret-ingest.ts` (`ingestStagingConnectorSecret`) + threading a grammar-safe `correlation_id` into the existing store audit + `scripts/check-no-real-tokens.sh`. **No migration, no schema/types/RLS change** (tests 571 → **585**; RLS still **611**; types **1901**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **Build split (doc 44 §7, B1/B2):** B1 builds the store/encrypt half with **synthetic sentinel values only**; the OAuth `oauth.v2.access` exchange + the first real-token event are **deferred to B2** (token born server-side, no human handling — doc 44's no-human-handling property preserved). **Merging B1 does NOT authorize a real-token run.**
- **Ingestion wrapper (`connector-secret-ingest.ts`):** the smallest guarded entry that encrypts + stores through the existing `saveConnectorSecret` (encrypt-only provider → atomic store + audit). Fail-closed guards IN ORDER: (1) **production hard-block** `isStagingIngestEnvironment()` — requires the explicit `CONNECTOR_VAULT_STAGING_INGEST_ENABLED=1` opt-in AND a non-production env (`VERCEL_ENV`/`NODE_ENV` ≠ production); fail-closed default-off; read from the trusted server env, never request data; (2) **provider/kind allowlist** — Slack dev-workspace bot OAuth access token ONLY; (3) required `tenant_id`/`connector_id` (uuid) + positive-int `version`; (4) **grammar-safe `correlation_id`** (reuses the #166 grammar via the new `isSafeCorrelationId`). On success → encrypt-immediately + atomic store + `store.attempted/succeeded` audit (carrying the correlation_id) + a redacted `SavedSecretRef`. **Plaintext is never logged/echoed/returned**; on any guard/encrypt/audit failure NOTHING commits (no secret row, no succeeded audit) and a static error is thrown.
- **Plaintext handling (doc 44 §2):** B1 has NO HTTP route, so there is no request-body observer surface (middleware/logger/tracer/body-parser) — the receipt-to-encryption observer analysis lands with B2's callback route. The wrapper receives the plaintext as a function arg and hands it straight to the encrypt call; the live reference is dropped after encrypt, V8 heap residual remains (NOT a hard wipe), exactly as doc 44 §2 step 7 documents.
- **Secret scanner (`scripts/check-no-real-tokens.sh`):** scans **changed files** (incl. untracked; `--all` for the whole tree) for HIGH-CONFIDENCE full-token shapes — Slack `xox[baprs]-`/`xapp-`, JWTs, `AKIA`/`ASIA`, PEM private keys, `gho_`/`github_pat_`, `sk-`/`sk-proj-`, and DB URLs with an embedded password to a non-local, non-`example.` host — redacts matches, exits non-zero on a hit, and **fails loud** if perl is absent. Decisions are **token-scoped** (a match is excused only if the matched TOKEN itself carries a sentinel marker — `SENTINEL`/`EXAMPLE`/`LEAK…`), NOT line-scoped, so a real token is still caught on a JSX/generic/`// placeholder` line; **no whole-file allowlist** (only the scanner skips itself), so a real token in any file is caught. A `selftest` subcommand proves 7 positives are caught (incl. real tokens with a sentinel marker sitting **adjacent** — proving the excuse is token-intrinsic, NOT proximity-based) + 6 sentinels excused only by their own intrinsic marker. **Wired into CI** (`review-discipline.yml`: `selftest` + `--all`).
- **Tests (synthetic sentinels only):** allowed ingestion stores envelope-only + no token anywhere (row/ref/audits/console); forbidden provider/kind rejected; missing/invalid ids + version rejected; invalid correlation_id rejected; **production guard refuses** (no row, no audit, no DB touch, returns nothing); audit-failure fails closed (atomic rollback, no secret row); encryption-failure fails closed; the module imports only sibling vault modules and contains no `fetch`/decrypt/`oauth.v2.access`/Slack-API/route/`xoxb-`.
- **Posture:** **B1 is synthetic-only and authorizes no real-token run.** NO real provider token. NO operator/admin-console paste. NO OAuth exchange. NO Slack API call. NO callback route. NO live connector. NO request-path decrypt. NO route returning a decrypted token. NO production enablement. NO rotation helper. Real credential save/load/use is still missing; the first real-token event is deferred to B2. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 44 (§2/§7), 42 (§89), 41 (§98), 09, 00.
- **Validation (local):** `npm test` **585/585**; lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean; `check-auth-safety`/`check-migration-safety`/`check-docs-updated`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `test-rls.sh` **611** (unchanged — no migration); `gen-types-local.sh` 0-diff (1901); `check-no-real-tokens.sh` passes; ref staging `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

A **nonexistent target → the helper THROWS** (caller NEVER receives `{ ok }`); NO lifecycle row, NO succeeded; `attempted` + `failed` ARE audited (the failed attempt is auditable). The **orphan invariant binds lifecycle ROWS** (never reference a nonexistent version), NOT the attempted/failed AUDIT rows. One statement → any insert failure rolls back the WHOLE statement (no lifecycle row without its audit, no compensating DELETE) → fail closed. Helpers return `{ ok }` / throw — never a secret/envelope/key material.
- **T54 — real DB, under `set role connector_runner`:** (1) GRANT SHAPE (exactly the 8 INSERT cols; no table-level INSERT; no UPDATE/DELETE; can't INSERT id/created_at/audit_log_id; `connector_secrets` still no runner UPDATE/DELETE); (2) APPEND-ONLY STILL HOLDS now that the runner has INSERT (runner UPDATE/DELETE fail; the trigger STILL rejects a privileged role's UPDATE/DELETE; row unchanged/still exists after); (3) the ACTUAL CTE — EXISTING v55 → lifecycle + attempted + succeeded, no failed, one terminal; NONEXISTENT v777 → attempted + failed(`target_not_found`), NO lifecycle/succeeded, one terminal; (4) ATOMICITY — a forced in-CTE succeeded-audit failure rolls back the lifecycle insert.
- **Posture:** **revoke/tombstone write helpers implemented (atomic with audit, exactly one terminal, nonexistent fails explicitly, no orphan lifecycle rows). NO rotation helper. No real provider token. No OAuth/token exchange. No live connector. No request-path decrypt. No service-role secret path. No public route. No UPDATE/DELETE on `connector_secrets` or the lifecycle table. No hard delete.** The #169 load semantics are unchanged. Real credentials remain blocked; real credential save/load/use is still missing; rotation + the rest of the real credential lifecycle remain missing. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§87), 41 (§96), 03, 09, 00 + `rls_test_plan.md`.

- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation (read-only). - **Lifecycle table (`0032`, INSERT-only):** `connector_secret_lifecycle_events` holds ONLY non-secret metadata (tenant/connector/secret_kind/version, `lifecycle_event_type` ∈ revoked/tombstoned/superseded, `reason_class`, actor_type, correlation_id, optional `audit_log_id`, created_at) — NO plaintext/ciphertext/DEK/key-material/AEAD-tag/nonce/aad_digest/KMS-response/raw-error/env. New `findLatestEncryptedSecret` resolves the **highest version across ALL rows first**, then loads **that single version's** eligibility — **NEVER falls back to a lower version** (highest revoked/tombstoned/expired with a lower valid → null; all revoked → null). Unit tests prove the adapter queries ONLY the highest version (no fallback) and that the no-lifecycle path is unchanged + all existing #167 store/load tests still pass.
- **T53 — TWO SEPARATE append-only proofs (per the amendment):** (1) the runner GRANT SHAPE under `set role connector_runner` (SELECT-only; INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE all FAIL); (2) the append-only TRIGGER itself, proven against a PRIVILEGED test/admin role that CAN attempt UPDATE/DELETE and is STILL rejected by the trigger (asserting the `append-only` message — so the block is the trigger, not mere grant-absence). Plus: the lifecycle-aware SELECT excludes an active version with a revoked event; `connector_secrets` still no runner UPDATE/DELETE.
- **Posture:** **Model B lifecycle table implemented; load is fail-closed latest-intent; runner has SELECT-only lifecycle access. Lifecycle write helpers, the runner INSERT grant, and lifecycle audit events/writes are deferred to the next PR. No real provider token storage. No OAuth/token exchange. No live connector. No request-path decrypt. No service-role secret path. No public route. No UPDATE/DELETE on `connector_secrets`. No hard delete.** Real credentials remain blocked; real credential save/load/use is still missing; rotation/revocation behavior + the real credential lifecycle remain missing. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§86), 41 (§95), 03, 09, 00 + `rls_test_plan.md`.
- **Validation (local, verified):** `npm test` **559/559**; lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean (18 routes); `check-auth-safety`/`check-migration-safety`/`check-docs-updated`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `test-rls.sh` **571** (`ALL ORG-RLS ASSERTIONS PASSED`); `gen-types-local.sh` regenerated (1901 — the new lifecycle table); linked ref confirmed `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

### PR #168 — Decide connector secret lifecycle data model (Model B) · 2026-06-24
- **Category:** design/spec — **docs-only.** No migration, no code, no test, no runtime event constant, no dependency. Tests unchanged 548; RLS unchanged 550; types unchanged 1837; no doc 17 §5 box ticked. **Resolves the rotation/revocation/tombstone data model and gives the next implementation PR exact instructions; implements NO lifecycle behavior.**
- **DECISION — Model B (doc 42 §85):** lifecycle state lives in a **separate, INSERT-only `connector_secret_lifecycle_events` table**, NOT in a mutable `connector_secrets.status`. **`connector_secrets` stays append-only — no UPDATE, no DELETE, no new UPDATE grant** (the RISK-007 invariant T50 protects). Rotation = a new higher-`version` `connector_secrets` row (immutable old row); revocation/tombstone = an insert-only lifecycle event; revoke-without-replacement is supported.
- **Lifecycle monotonicity:** events are monotonic toward non-loadable — any `revoked` or `tombstoned` event permanently makes that `(tenant, connector, secret_kind, version)` non-loadable; there is NO unrevoke/reactivate/restore event; a terminal non-loadable event always wins.
- **Load query — fail-closed latest-INTENT (the load-bearing rule):** exact-version lookup returns the secret only if that exact version is eligible (active, non-expired, well-formed, no revoked/tombstoned event), else null. Latest-version lookup (if added) computes the **highest version across ALL rows first** (including revoked/tombstoned/superseded/expired/malformed), then evaluates **that single highest version's** eligibility — **NO fallback to a lower version; NOT "highest eligible version."** Expiry is treated as strictly as revocation (highest expired + lower valid → null). Rationale: fallback can resurrect an old credential after an intended revoke/failed rotation/expiry. All edge cases enumerated in §85.6.
- **Grants/RLS spec (for the implementation PR, not done here):** `connector_secrets` keeps NO UPDATE/DELETE grant; the lifecycle table gets narrow column-scoped grants — `connector_runner` INSERT (write events) + SELECT (eligibility checks in the load query), **never UPDATE/DELETE**; an append-only trigger rejects UPDATE/DELETE proven under `set role connector_runner`; RLS proves tenant isolation; no service-role path, no public route.
- **Atomicity spec (for the implementation PR):** each lifecycle mutation + its audit event commit atomically in ONE runner transaction (the §84 pattern); audit failure rolls back the lifecycle change and vice-versa; NO compensating DELETE; real DB tests must prove all rollback cases under the runner.
- **Audit event names RESERVED in prose only** (`connector_secret.{rotation,revocation,tombstone}.{attempted,succeeded,failed}`) — **NOT added as runtime constants** (the #166 builder still supports only the nine store/load/decrypt events; #167 wired only the six store/load events; decrypt + lifecycle events remain unwired).
- **Posture:** **No lifecycle behavior is implemented yet. Real connector credential storage/use is still NOT allowed; rotation/revocation behavior and the real credential lifecycle remain missing. Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§85), 41 (§94), 09, 00.
- **Validation (local, verified):** `npm test` 548/548; lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `check-docs-updated`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `gen-types-local.sh` 0-diff (1837); linked ref confirmed `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); docs-only diff.

### PR #167 — Wire connector secret store/load audit emission (atomic, fail-closed) · 2026-06-24
- **Category:** RISK-007 audit wiring. Wires the #166 allowlist audit builder into the #160 runner-backed store adapter so store/load operations emit a **persisted** audit row, **fail-closed**. Adds migration **`0031`** (narrow audit-INSERT grant) + RLS **T52**; new `src/lib/server/connector-vault/secret-audit-writer.ts` (pure statement-builder) + rewritten `connector-secret-store.ts` (transactional + audited) + comprehensive tests; registers the writer in `no-client-import.test.ts`. Tests 539 → **548**; RLS suite **525 → 550** (T52); generated types unchanged 1837; migration `0031`; no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **STORE is ATOMIC (the amendment's hard requirement):** `insertEncryptedSecret` runs the `connector_secrets` INSERT and its `connector_secret.store.succeeded` audit INSERT in ONE runner transaction (`begin … commit`) on ONE connection as `connector_runner`. The secret row commits ONLY if its audit row commits; if the audit INSERT fails the WHOLE transaction rolls back — **no orphaned, unaudited secret, and NO compensating DELETE.** `store.attempted` (before) and `store.failed` (on a rolled-back failure) are audit-only writes, each fail-closed. **LOAD is fail-closed by ordering:** `findEncryptedSecret` writes `load.succeeded` (or `load.failed` with a static class) before returning; if the audit write fails the load throws and the caller receives **no** secret/envelope.
- **The audit writer ENLISTS in the runner transaction** — `secret-audit-writer.ts` turns a #166 record into the `audit_logs` INSERT `{sql, params}` the store splices into its own `runSequence` (same connection/role/tx). It opens NO connection, uses NO separate role, owns NO RPC/transaction (the amendment's precondition 2).
- **Migration `0031` — smallest safe grant:** COLUMN-scoped INSERT for `connector_runner` on `public.audit_logs`, EXACTLY `(tenant_id, action, resource_type, after_json)`. audit_logs ONLY, INSERT ONLY; no select/update/delete/truncate; no `actor_user_id`/`before_json`/`resource_id`/`ip_address`/`user_agent`; no other table broadened (connector_secrets still no UPDATE/DELETE). The 0002 append-only trigger still blocks mutation for the runner. **T52 proves** the grant shape + the atomic commit (both rows) + the atomic rollback (audit failure → no connector_secrets row, no compensating delete) + append-only — all under the real `set role connector_runner`.
- **Allowlist glue:** the adapter passes the #166 builder ONLY the explicit identity it picks (`tenant_id`, `connector_id`, `secret_kind`, `version`, actor `connector_runner`, static `error_class`) — never the raw input/`encrypted` envelope. A hostile store input (secret-shaped fields) AND a benign `favoriteColor` are both structurally dropped (tested). The audit row is the #166 allowlist `after_json` only; a failed audit write surfaces a redacted static error (no secret material).
- **Scope honesty:** only the six store/load events are wired; **decrypt audit remains BUILDER-ONLY** (no decrypt call site exists); **no `rotation`/`revocation`/`delete`/`update` event** is introduced. Synthetic DB shape proven by #163; KMS/IAM decrypt separation proven by #165. **No real provider token storage. No OAuth/token exchange. No live connector execution. No request-path decrypt. No service-role secret path. No API route for secret save/load/decrypt. No UPDATE/DELETE on `connector_secrets`. No rotation/revocation.** Real credentials still blocked; rotation/revocation + real credential lifecycle remain missing. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§84), 41 (§93), 03, 09, 00 + `rls_test_plan.md`.

### PR #166 — Add connector secret lifecycle audit scaffolding · 2026-06-24
- **Category:** RISK-007 audit scaffolding. Adds `src/lib/server/connector-vault/secret-audit.ts` (a pure, server-only, ALLOWLIST-based connector-secret lifecycle audit-event **builder**) + `secret-audit.test.ts` (load-bearing redaction tests); registers the module in `no-client-import.test.ts`. **No migration, no DB grant change, no RLS change, no write wiring, no dependency, no route.** Tests 528 → **539** (11 new); RLS unchanged 525; types unchanged 1837; no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **Honest scope (builder + tests only):** the #160 store adapter has no real call sites and there is no real credential lifecycle, so this PR wires NO writer and emits NO audit row — it adds the pure builder + tests only. A future server-only writer (when a real lifecycle exists) maps the builder output 1:1 onto the existing append-only `audit_logs` table (`action`/`resource_type`/`tenant_id`/`after_json`). **No migration** — `audit_logs` already supports these as `action` strings + an allowlisted `after_json`. **Does not claim the real credential lifecycle emits audit events (none exists).**

- **Allowlist, not denylist (the load-bearing property):** `buildConnectorSecretAuditEvent` constructs `after_json` from an EXPLICIT permitted-field set and never spreads the input (no metadata passthrough), so any extra property on a hostile input is structurally DROPPED. Allowed only: `event`, `tenant_id`, `connector_id`, `secret_kind`, `version`, derived `result`, optional `actor_type` (allowlist `connector_runner`), optional static `error_class` (fixed allowlist; unknown → `unknown_error`; `.failed` only), optional safe-shaped `correlation_id`. Identity fields uuid-shaped; `secret_kind` bounded snake token; every emitted string value re-scanned with the shared credential-value guard. Hard-prohibited (dropped/rejected): plaintext, provider/refresh/access token, client secret, ciphertext, DEK/wrapped DEK, key material, AEAD tag, nonce/IV, `aad_digest`, KMS response body, DB URL, env values, raw error object, arbitrary metadata. The redaction tests feed an intentionally hostile object with all of these and assert none survive (output, thrown errors, snapshots), that unknown fields are dropped (not redacted-in-place), that error handling records only a safe static class, that the event type is restricted to the nine (rotation/revocation rejected), and that the source opens no DB/provider/route/service-role path.
- **Status:** prior synthetic evidence stands — **synthetic DB shape proven by #163; synthetic KMS/IAM decrypt separation proven by #165.** This PR adds audit scaffolding only. **No real provider token storage. No OAuth/token exchange. No live connector execution. No request-path decrypt. No service-role secret path. No API route for secret save/load/decrypt. No UPDATE/DELETE on `connector_secrets`. No rotation/revocation events or behavior.** Real credentials are still blocked; **rotation/revocation and the real credential lifecycle remain missing.** **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§83), 41 (§92), 09, 00.

### PR #160 — Add runner-backed connector_secrets store adapter · 2026-06-24
- **Category:** wires the vault save/load boundary to the real `connector_secrets` table over the complete `0030` envelope (RISK-007 foundation). **Server-only `connector-secret-store.ts` + `connector-secret-store.test.ts` (10 tests) + RLS T51 + `no-client-import` guard hint.** No migration, no dependency; **no service-role client, no browser/request-path access, no public route, no OAuth exchange, no real provider token stored, no Okta live client, no UPDATE/DELETE on connector_secrets, no hosted command.** Tests 489 → **499**; RLS **522 → 525** (T51; T50 unchanged); types unchanged 1837 (no migration); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **What it implements** (doc 42 §78, doc 41 §89): `createRunnerConnectorSecretStore` implements the existing injected `ConnectorSecretWriteStore`/`ConnectorSecretReadStore` (secret-vault.ts) against `connector_secrets`, ONLY through the runner DB client path — every statement under **`SET ROLE connector_runner`** via the injected `RunnerConnection` (wrapped by `createRunnerDbClient`, which prepends the SET ROLE + redacts raw DB errors). Imports only `./runner-db-client` + `./secret-vault`; no Supabase client, no service-role, no `fetch`, no `process.env`, no route.
- **SAVE** inserts ONLY the 12 granted write columns — identity (`tenant_id`, `connector_id`, `secret_kind`, `version`) + the complete envelope (`ciphertext`, `dek_wrapped`, `aead_nonce`, `aad_digest`, `key_id`, `aead_tag`, `envelope_version`, `aead_alg`) via `encryptedSecretToColumns` — `RETURNING id`, fully parameterized (`$1..$12`); never `id`/`is_active`/`status`/`created_at`/`revoked_at`. The result is the row id ONLY — **no plaintext, no ciphertext**. **LOAD** selects ONLY granted columns, filters to one ACTIVE, non-expired row for (tenant, connector, kind, version) (`status = 'active' and (expires_at is null or expires_at > now())`), reconstructs the complete envelope via `columnsToEncryptedSecret`. **NO UPDATE, NO DELETE.**
- **Fail-closed:** a missing insert id, MORE THAN ONE matching active row (`limit 2`), or an INCOMPLETE/unsupported stored envelope (the mapper rejects a pre-`0030`/partial or non-v1/non-AES row) all throw a typed, secret-free error; a no-match load returns `null`. Unit tests prove SET-ROLE + parameterized SQL, only-allowed-columns (no `is_active`/`created_at`/`revoked_at`, no `id` in the insert list, no UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE), no-plaintext/ciphertext result, complete-envelope round-trip + decrypt, and the purity surface. **T51** runs the adapter's EXACT SELECT shape as `connector_runner` on real Postgres — proving every projected/filtered column (incl. `status`/`expires_at`) is grant-accessible under the `0029`/`0030` COLUMN grant AND the active/expiry/status filter is correct; **T50 is unchanged**.
- **RISK-007 remains OPEN.** This is the DB read/write adapter ONLY — NOT hosted KMS/IAM separation (mock KMS only, no real `KmsClient`), real end-to-end hosted runner secret write/load, audit, rotation/revocation, live provider token storage, or cutover readiness. **No service-role path is added. ### PR #157 — Complete connector secret encrypted envelope schema · 2026-06-23
- **Category:** completes the at-rest encrypted-envelope SHAPE for `connector_secrets` (RISK-007 foundation). **Migration `0030` (3 nullable envelope columns + extended `connector_runner` column-scoped grant) + `secret-vault.ts` column mappers + `secret-vault.test.ts` (2 new round-trip tests) + RLS T50 (extended) + verifier column-list update + regenerated types + docs.** No dependency; **no real KMS client, no live provider call, no OAuth exchange, no real token stored, no service-role path, no route/UI, no hosted command.** Tests 487 → **489**; RLS **519 → 522**; types **1828 → 1837** (the 3 new columns); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **What it implements** (doc 42 §77, doc 41 §88): #154 added the vault boundary, but `connector_secrets` (0017) had columns for only 5 of the 8 `EncryptedConnectorSecret` fields (crypto.ts). Migration `0030` adds the 3 missing columns — **`aead_tag`** (the 16-byte GCM auth tag, REQUIRED to decrypt; `tag`), **`envelope_version`** (`v`), **`aead_alg`** (`alg`) — so every envelope field now has a column and a real encrypted secret can be persisted + loaded as a COMPLETE envelope. Mapping: `ciphertext→ciphertext, wrappedDek→dek_wrapped, iv→aead_nonce, aadDigest→aad_digest, kekId→key_id` (0017) + the 3 new.
- **Non-destructive + column-scoped (no broadening).** The 3 columns are NULLABLE with NULL-permissive CHECKs (aead_tag = 16 bytes; envelope_version ≥ 1; aead_alg = `AES-256-GCM`); no column is dropped/renamed/retyped (no real secret was ever stored — #155/#156 verified the grant surface, not a write). `secret-vault.ts` adds pure `encryptedSecretToColumns`/`columnsToEncryptedSecret` mappers; a store-facing test round-trips a real payload (encrypt → columns → reconstruct byte-identical → decrypt → original plaintext) and `columnsToEncryptedSecret` FAILS CLOSED on an incomplete row (NULL tag/version/alg) or unsupported version/algorithm. The runner grant stays COLUMN-scoped (the #154 posture): `REVOKE ALL` then re-`GRANT SELECT/INSERT` the column sets EXTENDED with the 3 new columns — **NO table-level grant, NO UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER**; `authenticated`/`anon` keep EXACTLY zero table + column privilege; RLS-enabled, ZERO policies. T50 asserts the exact post-0030 column grants (15 SELECT / 12 INSERT) + the new aead_tag/envelope_version grants and functionally writes the complete envelope.
- **RISK-007 remains OPEN.** This completes the SCHEMA SHAPE for encrypted-envelope persistence ONLY — NOT hosted KMS/IAM separation, audit, rotation/revocation, or cutover readiness. The at-rest schema gap is now CLOSED, but remaining: the hosted KMS-grant runtime separation (mock KMS only, no real `KmsClient`); audited secret access/use; revocation/rotation/tombstone; staging verification of `0030`; production verification of `0030`; live token storage.
- **No real KMS client is added. No live Okta sync is added. This PR does not store real customer tokens. No service-role path is added. No provider API call is made. No OAuth code is exchanged for tokens. No access token is stored. No refresh token is stored. No API key is stored. No connector credentials are stored. No connector secret material is inserted, updated, deleted, or read in a hosted environment. No connector sync is implemented. No credential form is implemented. No connect/reconnect/disconnect action is exposed to users. No browser-accessible service-role request path is added. No hosted staging/production commands were run. Connector implementation remains blocked. Old-app parity is not complete. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§77), 41 (§88), 09, 00.

### PR #153 — Add Okta discovery fact emitter · 2026-06-23
- **Category:** the FIRST real provider-data mapper — **server-only `okta-discovery-emitter.ts` + `okta-discovery-emitter.test.ts` (26 tests) + the `no-client-import` guard hint + docs.** No dependency, **no migration**; **no canonical-graph/identity-match write, no service-role client, no provider API call, no token/credential, no `connector_secrets`, no HTTP route, no hosted command, no production mutation.** Tests 446 → **472**; RLS unchanged **492**; types unchanged 1828 (no schema change); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **Okta discovery fact emitter is added. Okta provider records are transformed into validated discovery facts. Okta facts are staged through the existing safe discovery fact pipeline** (doc 42 §75, doc 41 §86) — the PR #141 zod contract → the PR #142 `stageDiscoveryFactsForReview` RLS-backed staging path. High-risk (first provider data), so it maps records from an INJECTED source (real client wired later): **No live Okta sync is implemented. No provider API call is made in production.** Functions: `oktaApplicationToFacts` / `oktaUserToFacts` / `oktaAssignmentToFacts` / `emitOktaDiscoveryFacts`.
- Emits onto EXISTING schema vocabulary only: Okta app → `app_discovery` + `app_instance_identity` (external_instance_id = provider_app_id; instance_domain ONLY from explicit `settings.app.domain`); Okta user → `app_user_account` + `person_identity_candidate` (when email present); Okta assignment → `app_user_account` (user↔app, app_id_hint). `source_type` = `identity_provider_discovery`, `source_provider` = `okta`. **Okta facts do not write canonical graph records directly. Okta facts do not write app_user_identity_matches directly** — no direct write to apps/app_aliases/vendors/app_products/people/app_users/app_user_identity_matches.
- **ALLOWLIST construction (not blocklist stripping):** every fact is built from an explicit named allowlist of safe fields; the raw record is never spread; `settings` blob / `settings.signOn` (signing keys) / `_links` / `credentials` / `client_secret` / cookies / tokens are NEVER read — an unexpected or secret field cannot reach `fact_json`/`provenance_json` (tested). A domain/instance_domain is emitted ONLY from an explicit structured `settings.app.domain` field — never inferred from label/name/signOnMode/URL (negative test). Confidence is high (0.9) only because facts are anchored on deterministic Okta ids; `natural_key` derives from immutable Okta ids (stable to label/status changes). Email/login normalization REUSES `resolution.ts`'s `normalizeEmail` (trim + lowercase only — `Jane.Doe@Acme.com` == `jane.doe@acme.com`; `jane.doe` ≠ `janedoe`; `jane+test` ≠ `jane`).
- **Provider payload tenant_id is not trusted. Tenant scope comes from authenticated/server context** — every fact's tenant_id is the authenticated argument (a payload tenant_id is never read); `emitOktaDiscoveryFacts` stages nothing without an authenticated tenant; malformed records are skipped (fail closed). No `app_alias` fact type / `sso_app_id` alias / group-membership source plumbing is invented — documented as gaps for a later schema PR.
- **No OAuth code is exchanged for tokens. No access token is stored. No refresh token is stored. No API key is stored. No connector credentials are stored. No connector secret material is inserted, updated, deleted, or read. No connector sync is implemented. No credential form is implemented. No connect/reconnect/disconnect action is exposed to users. No browser-accessible service-role request path is added. No production data was touched. No hosted commands were run. Connector implementation remains blocked. Old-app parity is not complete. UI/UX parity is not complete. AI/API connector parity is not complete. Upload is not automatically production-ready. Hosted Auth/tenant-context is verified, but old-app replacement is not yet verified. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§75), 41 (§86), 09, 00.
